========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I guess I’m here because I can’t really get past something that’s been going on with my wife. She says I’m making too much of it, that the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, scheduling stuff, whatever. And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.

But for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.

I know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.

Like there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.

But for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.

I don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.

And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know my wife says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, but for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental, like there’s a private signal in them meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.

My wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.

Other people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.

But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know my wife says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, but for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental, like there’s a private signal in them meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.

But for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.

Not a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.

She says I’m making too much of it, that the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, scheduling stuff, whatever. And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know my wife says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, but for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental, like there’s a private signal in them meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.

For me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.

So it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.

And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know my wife says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, but for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental, like there’s a private signal in them meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.

It’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.

So when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.

But no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.

She says I’m making too much of it, that the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, scheduling stuff, whatever. And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.

It’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.

But over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.

So no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.

But when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.

So… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.

Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

There is no evidence of broader delusional themes, hallucinations, or functional impairment outside of this specific context, making F20 (Schizophrenia) less likely unless further symptoms emerge. F22 (Delusional Disorder) remains a strong candidate given the circumscribed nature of the belief.", "is_final": false}

I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know my wife says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, but for the past year they’ve lined up too precisely to feel accidental, like there’s a private signal in them meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. And I don’t think that happens.

For me it keeps coming back to my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in wording or tone in her messages are just normal office things. I can understand that explanation in a general way. I mean, I’m not unable to see that workplaces are messy and schedules shift.

But over the past year, some of it has lined up in a way that feels too exact to just brush off. It’s not that every message means something, but certain ones — the timing, the small changes, then a late meeting or some shift afterward — it feels like there’s a private signal in there, something meant for me to understand, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.

So if you mean outside of that, no, I can’t think of other examples. It’s really centered on her and those work-related things.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I can’t really get past something that’s been going on with my wife. She says I’m making too much of it, that the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, scheduling stuff, whatever. And I understand that’s what she says, I do. I’m not trying to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut for about the past year, the timing of it all has just been… too exact. Like these things line up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental to me. A late meeting happens right after a certain kind of message, or there’s a small change in wording, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Not obvious, not something you could point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but something meant to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain messages, actions, or events are directed at you in a personal way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, that’s probably the best way to put it. Not like billboards or TV messages or anything dramatic like that, but with my wife’s work messages and the way things happen afterward. She’ll say it’s just normal office coordination, and maybe to someone else it looks exactly like that, I understand that. But to me, the timing is too exact.\n\nLike there’ll be a certain wording in a message, something slightly changed from how she usually says it, and then suddenly there’s a late meeting, or she’s delayed, or some other small thing shifts. And when it keeps happening over and over for about a year, it starts to feel less like coincidence and more like… like there’s a private signal in it. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on, and not something I can lay out neatly as proof, but it feels directed toward me somehow. Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t.
