But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. I can understand that explanation in a general way. Outside of the situation with my wife, no, I don’t think so. I mean, I know the things I’m saying about her messages probably sound strange to other people. I’m aware of that. If someone else told me, “My wife’s late meetings and small wording changes in her work messages are signals,” maybe I’d have questions too.

But for me, it’s not like I have a whole set of beliefs like that about everything. It’s really this one area. My wife says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the changes in tone or wording are just normal office stuff, and I understand that’s the explanation other people accept. I’m not saying I have proof I could put on a table.

It’s just that over the past year, the timing has lined up too precisely for me to feel comfortable calling it accidental. Certain messages come through a little differently, or at a particular time, and then something shifts afterward, and it feels like there’s some private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.

So yes, I know others might consider that untrue or strange. But no, I can’t really think of beliefs like that outside of this situation with her.

But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. And I don’t think that happens.\n\nFor me it keeps coming back to my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in wording or tone in her messages are just normal office things. I can understand that explanation in a general way. I mean, I’m not unable to see that workplaces are messy and schedules shift.\n\nBut over the past year, some of it has lined up in a way that feels too exact to just brush off. It’s not that every message means something, but certain ones — the timing, the small changes, then a late meeting or some shift afterward — it feels like there’s a private signal in there, something meant for me to understand, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo if you mean outside of that, no, I can’t think of other examples. It’s really centered on her and those work-related things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that you know others would consider strange or untrue, even if they weren’t related to your wife’s messages or work events?\n\nPatient: Outside of the situation with my wife, no, I don’t think so. I mean, I know the things I’m saying about her messages probably sound strange to other people. I’m aware of that. If someone else told me, “My wife’s late meetings and small wording changes in her work messages are signals,” maybe I’d have questions too.\n\nBut for me, it’s not like I have a whole set of beliefs like that about everything. It’s really this one area. My wife says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the changes in tone or wording are just normal office stuff, and I understand that’s the explanation other people accept. I’m not saying I have proof I could put on a table.\n\nIt’s just that over the past year, the timing has lined up too precisely for me to feel comfortable calling it accidental. Certain messages come through a little differently, or at a particular time, and then something shifts afterward, and it feels like there’s some private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo yes, I know others might consider that untrue or strange.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not generally, no. I don’t walk around feeling like everyone is treating me differently, if that’s what you mean. At work or with neighbors or people in stores, I don’t really get that sense that they’re all behaving oddly toward me or sending me some kind of message.

The place where I get stuck is with my wife, and really with the things around her work. I know she says the late meetings are just normal office stuff, and that small changes in wording in her messages don’t mean anything. I do understand that’s how she sees it, and I know other people would probably say the same thing.

But over the past year, the timing of some of it has felt too exact to just ignore. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some other change, and it feels like there’s something in it — not something obvious, but almost like a private signal. Like she’s trying to tell me something indirectly, something she won’t just say out loud.

So, no, I wouldn’t say people around me in general act differently for no reason. It’s more that these specific things with her have started to feel meaningful in a way I can’t quite shake.

A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. And I don’t think that happens.\n\nFor me it keeps coming back to my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in wording or tone in her messages are just normal office things. I can understand that explanation in a general way. I mean, I’m not unable to see that workplaces are messy and schedules shift.\n\nBut over the past year, some of it has lined up in a way that feels too exact to just brush off. It’s not that every message means something, but certain ones — the timing, the small changes, then a late meeting or some shift afterward — it feels like there’s a private signal in there, something meant for me to understand, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo if you mean outside of that, no, I can’t think of other examples. It’s really centered on her and those work-related things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that you know others would consider strange or untrue, even if they weren’t related to your wife’s messages or work events?\n\nPatient: Outside of the situation with my wife, no, I don’t think so. If someone else told me, “My wife’s late meetings and small wording changes in her work messages are signals,” maybe I’d have questions too.\n\nBut for me, it’s not like I have a whole set of beliefs like that about everything. Certain messages come through a little differently, or at a particular time, and then something shifts afterward, and it feels like there’s some private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo yes, I know others might consider that untrue or strange. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I’d call it a coordinated effort, exactly. Not like there’s a whole group of people involved or some big hidden system, no. That sounds larger than what I mean.

It’s more… specific to her. To my wife. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in her work messages are normal office stuff — different wording, different tone, someone’s busy, schedules change. I can say all of that back, and I understand why someone would hear it that way.

But for the past year, certain things have lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It’s not every message, and it’s not like I’m decoding the whole world. It’s just that sometimes a message comes through at a certain time, or it’s worded in a slightly different way, and then there’s a late meeting or some shift afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.

So, no, I wouldn’t say it’s a larger system that others don’t understand. But I do feel like there’s something in those messages meant for me to pick up on, even if other people would just say I’m reading too much into it.

Like she’s telling me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like messages or events, have a hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s really the thing I keep coming back to. I know my wife says it’s just normal work stuff—meetings running late, messages being phrased a little differently, schedules changing, all of that. And I can understand how, from the outside, someone might say, “Well, offices are like that, people get busy, meetings change.” I’m not blind to that.\n\nBut for the past year or so, it’s been the way those things line up. It’s not just one late meeting or one odd message. It’s the pattern. A message will have a slight change in tone or wording, something that feels just a little off from usual, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some delay. And when it happens again and again, it starts to feel like it’s not random.\n\nI don’t mean that everyone else would see it. They probably wouldn’t. But to me it feels like there’s a hidden meaning in it, like a private signal, almost as if she’s communicating something indirectly—something she won’t just say to me plainly. I know that sounds hard to prove, and that’s part of what makes it so frustrating, because I can’t point to one single thing and say, “That’s it.” It’s more the timing and the repetition of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or events are communicating something to you that you believe only you can understand or notice, in a way that others wouldn't pick up on?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s how it feels. I don’t mean it like some obvious code that anyone could look at and decode, or like she’s writing something directly to me. It’s more subtle than that, which is partly why it’s so hard to explain without sounding like I’m reaching.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I know that’s the reasonable explanation she gives. But over the past year, the way they’ve lined up has felt too precise to just brush off. A message will be worded a little differently, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like it’s meant to communicate something to me without her actually saying it out loud.\n\nOther people probably wouldn’t notice it, no. They’d probably just see work messages and scheduling. But I notice the pattern, or at least I feel like I do, and that’s what I can’t get past.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something others see as ordinary or neutral, like a message or event, was actually meant to communicate something important specifically to you, even if they couldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I know I’ve probably already said this a few different ways, but that’s really the part that bothers me most. My wife will say the messages from work are just ordinary things—someone changing a meeting time, wording something differently, her staying late because something came up—and I do understand that, on the surface, that’s what it looks like.\n\nBut for about a year now, the way those things happen together has felt too exact to me. It’s like a message will have a small change in wording, or it will come at a particular time, and then right after that there’s a late meeting or some shift in her plans. If it happened once or twice, I’d probably let it go. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nNot a public message, not something anyone else would notice. More like a private signal meant for me, like she’s trying to tell me something important without actually saying it directly. And that’s what makes it so hard, because other people would probably just see normal work stuff, but to me it doesn’t feel neutral anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public, like something on TV, in a conversation, or even a stranger’s actions, are somehow meant for you or contain a hidden message directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or strangers, no. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m watching the news or hearing people talk in a store and thinking they’re sending me a message. It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings and the little changes in wording are just normal office things, and I do understand that people get busy and schedules shift. But over the past year, those things have lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. Like a message will sound just slightly different, or come at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some change afterward.\n\nSo it’s not public things exactly. It’s more like there’s a private signal in those ordinary work details, something meant for me to notice, as if she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly. I know other people probably wouldn’t see it that way, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people other than your wife, or situations outside of work messages and meetings, were also sending you hidden signals or communicating with you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of that. I mean… I’ve thought about that, because I know how it must sound when I explain it, like maybe I’m seeing signals everywhere, but that isn’t how it is for me.\n\nIt’s not strangers, or television, or random conversations, or things like that. I don’t walk around thinking everyone is communicating with me in some hidden way. It’s much more specific. It’s about my wife’s work messages, the late meetings, the little changes in wording or timing, and how they keep lining up. She says it’s just normal office stuff, and I understand that explanation. I can repeat it back to myself. But over the past year, the pattern has felt too precise to feel accidental.\n\nSo when a message comes in a certain way, or there’s a small change in how something is phrased, and then it connects with her staying late or a meeting shifting, it feels like there’s something being communicated there. Not something anyone else would necessarily pick up on. More like a private signal, like she’s telling me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say other people are doing that to me. It’s really centered on her and those work situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns like this about anything else in your life, or is this something that only involves your wife’s work messages and the timing of events around them?\n\nPatient: It’s really only this, as far as I can tell. I don’t have the same feeling about other parts of my life. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the little wording changes in messages are just normal office communication. And I can understand that explanation when she says it. I’m not saying every single message means something.\n\nBut over the past year, the way some of it has lined up has felt too precise to me. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at an odd time, or something gets shifted, and then she stays late or a meeting changes, and it feels like there’s a connection there. Like there’s a private signal in it, something she’s trying to tell me without saying it directly.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say it’s spread into everything else. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. And I don’t think that happens.\n\nFor me it keeps coming back to my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in wording or tone in her messages are just normal office things. I can understand that explanation in a general way. I mean, I’m not unable to see that workplaces are messy and schedules shift.\n\nBut over the past year, some of it has lined up in a way that feels too exact to just brush off. It’s not that every message means something, but certain ones — the timing, the small changes, then a late meeting or some shift afterward — it feels like there’s a private signal in there, something meant for me to understand, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo if you mean outside of that, no, I can’t think of other examples. It’s really centered on her and those work-related things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that you know others would consider strange or untrue, even if they weren’t related to your wife’s messages or work events?\n\nPatient: Outside of the situation with my wife, no, I don’t think so. I mean, I know the things I’m saying about her messages probably sound strange to other people. I’m aware of that. If someone else told me, “My wife’s late meetings and small wording changes in her work messages are signals,” maybe I’d have questions too.\n\nBut for me, it’s not like I have a whole set of beliefs like that about everything. It’s really this one area. My wife says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the changes in tone or wording are just normal office stuff, and I understand that’s the explanation other people accept. I’m not saying I have proof I could put on a table.\n\nIt’s just that over the past year, the timing has lined up too precisely for me to feel comfortable calling it accidental. Certain messages come through a little differently, or at a particular time, and then something shifts afterward, and it feels like there’s some private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo yes, I know others might consider that untrue or strange. But no, I can’t really think of beliefs like that outside of this situation with her.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people around you were acting differently toward you in ways you couldn’t explain, even if there was no clear reason for them to do so?\n\nPatient: Not generally, no. I don’t walk around feeling like everyone is treating me differently, if that’s what you mean. At work or with neighbors or people in stores, I don’t really get that sense that they’re all behaving oddly toward me or sending me some kind of message.\n\nThe place where I get stuck is with my wife, and really with the things around her work. I know she says the late meetings are just normal office stuff, and that small changes in wording in her messages don’t mean anything. I do understand that’s how she sees it, and I know other people would probably say the same thing.\n\nBut over the past year, the timing of some of it has felt too exact to just ignore. A message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a certain time, and then there’s a late meeting or some other change, and it feels like there’s something in it — not something obvious, but almost like a private signal. Like she’s trying to tell me something indirectly, something she won’t just say out loud.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say people around me in general act differently for no reason. It’s more that these specific things with her have started to feel meaningful in a way I can’t quite shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or signals from your wife are part of something larger, like a coordinated effort or a hidden system that you're meant to understand but others don't?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it a coordinated effort, exactly. Not like there’s a whole group of people involved or some big hidden system, no. That sounds larger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more… specific to her. To my wife. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in her work messages are normal office stuff — different wording, different tone, someone’s busy, schedules change. I can say all of that back, and I understand why someone would hear it that way.\n\nBut for the past year, certain things have lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It’s not every message, and it’s not like I’m decoding the whole world. It’s just that sometimes a message comes through at a certain time, or it’s worded in a slightly different way, and then there’s a late meeting or some shift afterward, and it feels like there’s a private signal in it. Like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it’s a larger system that others don’t understand. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think it’s an organized plan or scheme. I know that probably sounds like the next step people assume I mean, but I really don’t think there are organizations involved, or other people working together in some coordinated way.

What I mean is more limited than that. It’s about my wife and the messages from her work, the timing of things, the late meetings, the slight changes in how something is worded. She tells me those are just normal office things — people get busy, meetings run late, messages sound different depending on the day — and I understand that explanation. I really do.

But over the past year, it’s just felt too precise sometimes. Like a message will come through at a certain time, or with a slightly different tone, and then afterward something changes, or there’s another late meeting, and it feels like there’s something underneath it. Not a big network or some outside group, but more like a private signal from her. Like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it directly.

So no, not a larger organized plan. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some of these things are meant for me to notice.

It’s not like that.\n\nFor me it’s much more tied to my wife and her work messages, and the timing around them. I don’t think, say, my students’ emails or something happening on the street is meant for me in that way.\n\nIt’s specifically with my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. It’s mainly—really only—around her work and those patterns.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, despite evidence to the contrary, that these patterns in your wife’s messages and events are definitely not random and must mean something specific, even if others can’t see it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “definitely” in the sense that I can prove it to someone else, because I know I can’t. That’s part of what makes it hard to talk about. If I lay it out to another person, they can always say, “Well, meetings run late, people phrase emails differently, work schedules change,” and yes, I understand that. I really do.\n\nBut when it’s happening in my own house, with my wife, and I’m seeing the timing of it over and over for the past year, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too exact. Like a message comes in with a slightly different tone, or at a strange time, or there’s a small change in how something is said, and then there’s a late meeting or something shifts afterward. One time by itself, fine, that could be nothing. But when it keeps lining up, it starts to feel like there’s something underneath it.\n\nSo… yes, I do think it means something. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff, and maybe other people wouldn’t see any pattern there. But to me it feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it straight out. I don’t know if I’d use the word “must” every single time, but I have a very hard time believing it’s all just accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages and events involving your wife are the only instances where things seem to have a hidden meaning for you, or have you ever felt similarly about other areas of your life, even if you didn’t act on those feelings?\n\nPatient: I think it’s only with my wife, yes. At least, that’s the honest answer as I understand it. I’ve tried to check myself on that, because I know if I said, “Everything has hidden meaning,” that would be a very different thing. But I don’t feel that way about everything.\n\nIt’s really the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages, the timing of them, the way certain things seem to come together. She tells me it’s just normal office stuff, and I know that’s the simple explanation. I’m not saying I don’t understand that. But over the past year, it’s been hard for me to accept that it’s all just random, because some of it has lined up too precisely.\n\nIt feels like there’s a private signal in it, like something meant for me to pick up on, something she won’t just say directly. Not like a message from strangers or from other parts of my life. I don’t really get that feeling elsewhere. It’s centered on her and those situations.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences, even small ones, where you felt something was meant for you or had a special meaning — even if it wasn’t about your wife or these work messages?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve thought about that, actually, because I know what you’re asking — whether I get that feeling in other places too, like a song on the radio or something someone says in passing, or some random event meaning something special to me. And I don’t think that happens.\n\nFor me it keeps coming back to my wife’s work messages and the timing around them. I know she says the late meetings are just late meetings, and that the little changes in wording or tone in her messages are just normal office things. I can understand that explanation in a general way. I mean, I’m not unable to see that workplaces are messy and schedules shift.\n\nBut over the past year, some of it has lined up in a way that feels too exact to just brush off. It’s not that every message means something, but certain ones — the timing, the small changes, then a late meeting or some shift afterward — it feels like there’s a private signal in there, something meant for me to understand, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo if you mean outside of that, no, I can’t think of other examples. It’s really centered on her and those work-related things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences that you know others would consider strange or untrue, even if they weren’t related to your wife’s messages or work events?\n\nPatient: Outside of the situation with my wife, no, I don’t think so. I mean, I know the things I’m saying about her messages probably sound strange to other people. I’m aware of that. If someone else told me, “My wife’s late meetings and small wording changes in her work messages are signals,” maybe I’d have questions too.\n\nBut for me, it’s not like I have a whole set of beliefs like that about everything. It’s really this one area. My wife says the late meetings are just late meetings, and the changes in tone or wording are just normal office stuff, and I understand that’s the explanation other people accept. I’m not saying I have proof I could put on a table.\n\nIt’s just that over the past year, the timing has lined up too precisely for me to feel comfortable calling it accidental. Like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nSo yes, I know others might consider that untrue or strange. I don’t walk around feeling like everyone is treating me differently, if that’s what you mean. At work or with neighbors or people in stores, I don’t really get that sense that they’re all behaving oddly toward me or sending me some kind of message.\n\nThe place where I get stuck is with my wife, and really with the things around her work. Like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it’s a larger system that others don’t understand. I wouldn’t say I think she’s trying to control me, exactly. That sounds stronger than what I mean. It’s not like I think she’s planting thoughts in my head or trying to make me do specific things.

I really do.\n\nBut over the past year, it’s just felt too precise sometimes. Like a message will come through at a certain time, or with a slightly different tone, and then afterward something changes, or there’s another late meeting, and it feels like there’s something underneath it. Not a big network or some outside group, but more like a private signal from her. Like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it directly.\n\nSo no, not a larger organized plan. That sounds stronger than what I mean. It’s not like I think she’s planting thoughts in my head or trying to make me do specific things.\n\nBut… yes, I do think there’s some intent behind it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think deliberately, though I’m careful with that word because I know how it sounds. I don’t mean she’s sitting there planning some elaborate thing, or that every message from her work has a hidden meaning. It’s not like that.

But there have been too many times over the past year where the timing or the wording of something felt… too exact. Like she’ll say a meeting ran late, or a work message will come through with a slightly different tone, and I can tell myself, “Okay, that’s normal, people get busy, messages sound different from day to day.” I do understand that explanation.

But I don’t fully believe it’s all accidental. Some of it feels like it’s meant for me to notice. Like there’s something she’s trying to communicate indirectly, something she won’t just say out loud to me. Not necessarily to control me or make me do anything, but to get a message across without admitting that’s what she’s doing.

So yes, I suppose I do believe that some of those signals are deliberate. Not all of them, but enough that I can’t just dismiss it.
